- LEDGER.md:178 (row 133): | 133 | **Stability at six meets at one seam. R's reduction: given the quantified cone lemma, `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≥ 7` (row 131's C-Q-STAB, disc form) follows from (A) a complementary pair of fat junctions at distance in `[η, 2 − η]`, blocking toward a common unit point, in every `U_R` of every band-proper partial six-colouring with small holes, and (B) a lower bound on their cone scale — for two such junctions force `|H ∩ B(w, µ/4)| ≥ (t₁ − 2r)(t₁′ − 2r′)`, `t₁ = s·sin(µ/2)/3`, for every `ε ≥ 0`, repair-robust and with an uncovered disc, when `s, s′ ≤ µη²/100`, `2r < t₁` and each colour's arc meets the arc of half-width `π/2 − µ` about the direction (FS107-R-CP). W's equivalence: at bounded arc complexity `K`, band-proper designs with vanishing holes exist iff a full tight a.e.-proper six-colouring by a map of complexity `≤ K` exists (⟸ at `O(K)`, `δ(ε) = O(ε)`, periodised by a seam strip), and in such a map complementary-type vertices within distance 2 are tangent pairs or bowties — the cone lemma read as a design rule (W-TAN), a GAP at cusp vertices. So stability at six has two routes, each needing a third statement (row 139): route M, the map NO (FS-110's question, then FS-114's) (FS-110; YES gives `χ(ℝ²) ≤ 6` with no MR, NO answers `[SokolovVoronov2025]` Question 2 a.e. for locally finite arc-bounded maps) with the map step; route R, (A) at every complexity with (B), the map NO idle. No-gos: polygonal designs of bounded complexity keep holes `≥ δ₀(K)`, bar `d = 2` (W-POLY); an argument reading at a flat swap only the away-facing colour is capped at `2εs(1 + o(1))`, while the facing colour alone certifies `s³/256` (OB′ corrected; SW). Measured: the hexagonal seven-colouring with one class absorbed by straight triangles stalls at hole density `π/(42√3) ≈ 0.0432`, attained by no member (HX-STALL). It moves no value** (FS-107; FS107-RR, R-CP, W-EQ-K, W-TAN, W-POLY, R-SW, R-OB2, HX-STALL, R-SAT, W-RATE, V-C5, MEET). **[hand; RR after two repairs, CP by different routes, W-EQ-K, W-POLY, W-RATE, W-TAN and HX-STALL two minds, one blind (R and V; W and VW); SW's `s³` and OB′'s correction one mind (V); W-TAN over C-T-TAN, one mind (TS), a GAP at cusps; the meeting one mind (A2); HX-STALL exact in ℚ(√3) by two checkers]** Mechanism: a certificate on cones of scale `s` costs `≍ s²` whatever `ε`, so what a stability proof must bound is a feature's scale, not the band's (G82 as corrected); C-T-TAN is read from both sides, as an area on the sequence and as a rule on the limit. **Not claimed:** (A), (A″) or (B); C-VOID (RR does not give it); R″; any hole-vanishing family; W-TAN at cusps; FS107-V's C5, that tangent ring junctions still force holes at positive scale (one instance and a sketch, one mind). **Prior art:** not found in print (FS-107's gate); the qualitative cone lemma is `[Voronov2023]` Prop. 2.2's tangent form (FS106-TAN); the periodic approximation of one class is `[DeCorteOliveiraVallentin2022]` Lemma 6.2. | `plans/FS107-derivation.md`; `plans/FS107-R-stability.md` §3; `plans/FS107-W-near-colourings.md` §§2–3; `plans/FS107-V-verification.md`; `plans/FS107-VW-verification.md`; `tools/fs107_v_cp_check.py`, `tools/fs107_v_sw_check.py`, `tools/fs107_vw_hx_check.py`, `tools/fs107_vw_member_check.py` | FS-107 (2026-09-27) |

- plans/FS103-derivation.md:100 (after entry FS103-M1): **FS103-M1 — `χ_CP(ℝ²) ≤ χ_m⁰(ℝ²)`: a measurable `k`-colouring proper at almost every edge gives a CP `k`-colouring (the invariant measures on its translates' weak-* orbit closure in `L^∞(ℝ², Δ_k)` are continuous in probability for free; properness passes to the limit because `σ̂ = J₀ → 0`; one random threshold rounds `Δ_k` to `[k]`). General form: for a finite Rajchman `σ` on `ℝⁿ` and symmetric `T ⊆ supp σ`, `χ_CP(Cay(ℝⁿ, T)) ≤ χ_m⁰(ℝⁿ, σ)` — false without `T ⊆ supp σ` (FS103-M-NSUPP). [hand, two (M; N blind)]** *Proof:* M §2.2; N §2.1 (N-M1). *Proof verdict:* TRUE for ℝ², both; the general form TRUE with the support hypothesis (N). *Rests on it:* FS103-M1b, -M1c, -Q4; the C-FMT (c) and C-CPM verdicts (GAP → TRUE). *Prior art:* the gate (FS103-G §2.2): not found; `[BachocEtAl2014]` prints `χ(A) ≤ χ_m` for operators respecting `G`, not this; "a.e.-proper measurable colouring + Rajchman edge measure ⟹ stationary CP colouring" not searched (N) — a debt. *Kernel:* declined.

- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:238: | **C-AE-NICE** (the a.e. form of `[Thomassen1999]` Theorem 1 on the plane) | **its a.e. form is the theorem itself**: Thomassen's nice condition is already a condition on open faces ("each color class is the union of faces (and part of their boundaries) such that the distance between any two of these faces is greater than 1", faces of "diameter less than 1"), and his proof uses no boundary point's colour — only that a face and every face whose facial cycle meets its cycle are pairwise at distance `< 1` (checked: two such neighbours `y, z` of `x` have points `p ∈ C_y ∩ C_x`, `q ∈ C_z ∩ C_x` with `|p − q| ≤ diam cl F_x < 1`), so they form a clique of the colour-conflict graph, and a map-graph vertex of degree `≥ 6` (his Lemma 1 after the 2-separator surgery) gives 7 colours. So C-AE-NICE ⟺ TH-99 on the plane; its truth is TH-99's proof, whose verification is V3's. **The relaxation that matters is different**: faces of one colour with no unit pair between them (instead of distance `> 1`) is a subcase of C-AE-Q2, and there Thomassen's clique step fails — two small faces of one colour on the rim of a third face can be `< 1` apart with no unit pair (their union of diameter `< 1`) — so that form is **GAP with C-AE-Q2** | 1 / [hand] + [external, read once] | For the plane, (i)–(iii) of TH-99 hold; nice colourings are everywhere proper for every choice of boundary colours (a monochromatic unit pair on closures would put two faces of one colour at distance `≤ 1`). |
- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:240: | **C-AE-MAP6** (the a.e. form of `[Townsend2005]` / Townsend's manuscript: five colours) | **GAP, pending FS106-V3's reading of Townsend's proof; reduced here to one step** | 1 / [hand] | What holds with no Condition 5 or 6, five colours, a.e.-proper: TS-T gives a point `u` of chromaticity `≥ 3` within 1 of every point; region points of `ω(u)` avoid `c(u)` (stage 2 §2.5, row "Prop. 6, Lemma 2"), so they use at most two colours; a region arc of `ω(u)` of one colour longer than `π/3` carries a unit pair. **What is left is exactly Townsend's printed lemma in a.e. form** (TW-81: "any 2-colouring of A° contains a monochrome unit" for a "finitely disconnected unit annulus" with a cut-point-free segment of length `> π/3`): the two free colours must fill a deformed annulus about `ω(u)`, and unit-arc boundaries are what disconnect it (K2 is the reason the hypothesis has cut points). The exact-distance circle alone does not refute two colours (alternating arcs of `π/3`), so the thickening is essential. **"Boundaries of measure 0" is not used by any step written here**; it matters only for the link to `χ_m⁰` (a fat boundary is an uncoloured positive-measure set). |
- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:256: | **C-T-L1b** (no regularity) | **TRUE when the classes near `x` and `y` form a locally finite map (any vertex degree, corners and cones included: TS-L1 in D-NF, as above); GAP in general**, at two named steps: (1) the localisation — near `x`, classes outside `P(x)` have density 0 **at** `x` but may have density-1 points accumulating at `x`, so `ω(y) ∩ U(x)` need only avoid `S̃_a ∪ S̃_b`, not lie in an interface; (2) the collar — TS-U needs an arc of `ω(y)` with a one-sided open neighbourhood in each class, which a class with no regularity (K3's fat edge, or a comb) need not supply | 1 / [hand] | **What does hold with no regularity, at first order** [hand, one mind]: if along one sequence `r_n → 0` the rescaled classes at `x` and at `y` converge in `L¹_loc` (strong blow-ups `A^x_a ∪ A^x_b = ℝ²`, `A^y_a ∪ A^y_b = ℝ²` a.e.), then with `e = y − x` and `f^z_c(t)` the length of `A^z_c ∩ {p·e = t} ∩ B(0, R)`, the unit relation at scale `r_n` becomes `p·e = q·e + O(r_n)`, and the slice argument of C-T-TAN below gives `f^x_c(t) f^y_c(t) = 0` for a.e. `t` and each `c`; since the slices of `a` and `b` fill each line, **for a.e. `t` the line `{p·e = t}` is all `a` at `x` and all `b` at `y`, or the reverse**: the blow-ups are stripes perpendicular to `e`, complementary at the two ends (`A^x_a = {p·e ∈ T}`, `A^y_a = {q·e ∉ T}`). The half-plane case `T = (−∞, 0)` is the facing-away pair. **Second order kills it when the interfaces are graphs** `{p·e = g(p·e^⊥)}` near `x` and `{q·e = h(q·e^⊥)}` near `y` (exact unit relation `(p − q)·e = |p − q|²/2`): class `b` forces `g(s) − h(s′) ≥ (s − s′)²/2` and class `a` forces `≤`, so `g(s) − h(s′) = (s − s′)²/2` for all small `s, s′`; `s = s′` gives `g = h`, then `s′ = 0` gives `g(s) = s²/2` and `s′ = −s` gives `0 = 2s²` — contradiction. What is left open: blow-ups whose stripe set `T` is not a half-line, and classes whose blow-ups converge only weak-* (fractional limits), where the slice argument has no hold. |
- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:257: | **C-T-BIKE** | **GAP — undecided here**; the step named: an explicit proper 3-colouring of `T₁(u) ∪ T₁(v)` by finitely many arcs (or a proof none exists), for each `d = |u − v| ∈ (1, 2)` | 1 / [hand] | **Held:** (i) three colours are needed at every `d ∈ (1, 2)`: a point `x ∈ T₁(u)` with `|x − v| = √3` (exists, since `|x − v|` runs over `[d − 1, d + 1] ∋ √3`) has two partners on `T₁(v)` at distance exactly 1 from each other (two unit circles with centres `√3` apart meet at points 1 apart) — a triangle. (ii) The index obstruction of Voronov's Thm. 1.2 is gone at exact distance: its only use of the band is to exclude a simultaneous swap `(a, b) → (b, a)` of the two partner colours (FS106-V R7; FS106-VB control 3), and at exact distance a swap at an isolated parameter is harmless. (iii) **The reduction** (for a route, not a verdict): colour `T₁(u)` by `{1, 2}` in alternating half-open arcs of `π/3` with offset `φ`; a point `y ∈ T₁(v)` whose two partners (symmetric about the line `uy`, `2 arccos(|y − u|/2)` apart) get different colours is forced to colour 3 — call that set `F`; elsewhere `y` takes 3 or the colour its partners lack. The bicycle is 3-colourable by arcs of this shape iff for some `φ` the set `F` is independent on `T₁(v)` and the unforced points can be chosen around it — a one-dimensional problem on one circle per `(d, φ)`. Not solved here: choosing `φ` by a scan is a search (the ban). The registered expectation E9 (TRUE 0.7) stays unscored by this job. |

- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:270: | **C-T-TRIb** (support form) | **GAP**; one tool proved, the step named | 1 / [hand] | **Proved — TS-C′ (TS-C for supports):** if `K ⊆ supp S_i` is connected and the points of `K` where `S_i` has upper density 0 form a set `Z₀` with `H¹(Z₀) = 0`, then `diam K ≤ 1`. *Proof:* if `p, q ∈ K`, `|p − q| > 1`, pick `p′ ∈ S̃_i` near `p` with `ω(p′) ∩ Z₀ = ∅` — possible for a.e. `p′`, since `Z₀ + S¹` is a Lipschitz image of the `H²`-null set `Z₀ × S¹`, hence null; by I-D1 `ω(p′)` also misses every point of `K` of positive upper density, so it misses `K`; then `K ∪ [p′, p]` is connected, misses `ω(p′)`, and holds `p′` inside and `q` outside — contradiction. ∎ This is TS-C with "touching vertex" replaced by "zero-density point" and genericity by Fubini. **The named step:** TS-T's hull growth on supports — the outer frontier of a component of `{x : P_supp(x) = {j}}` is connected and lies in one `supp S_j ∩ supp S_i`, and the ring of `i` around it must be a connected piece of `supp S_i` to which TS-C′ applies. K3 shows where it breaks: `A`'s support is connected across `K × ℝ`, on which `A` has density 0 almost everywhere (`H¹(Z₀) = ∞`). |
- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:271: | **C-T-CHR4** (`k = 6`: no `|P(x)| ≥ 4`) | **GAP** — proved for `|P(x)| ≥ 5` off a null set; `|P(x)| = 4` open | 1 / [hand] | By I-D1, `ω(x) ∩ S̃_j = ∅` for `j ∈ P(x)`, and `S̃_j` has no two points at distance 1 (D-NF), so `σ(ω(x) ∩ S̃_j) ≤ 1/2` for each `j`. If `|P(x)| ≥ 5`, at most one class is left for `ω(x)`, so `σ(ω(x) ∩ E) ≥ 1/2`; if `|P(x)| = 6`, `ω(x) ⊆ E`. By Fubini (`E` null), `X_E = {x : σ(ω(x) ∩ E) > 0}` is null. **So `|P(x)| ≥ 5` happens only on the null set `X_E`.** For `|P(x)| = 4` off `X_E`, `ω(x)` is `σ`-a.e. split between two classes with no two points `π/3` apart — alternating arcs, which is consistent: the circle alone does not refute it. S–V's Lemma 2 gets its contradiction from Prop. 5 (the neighbourhoods of the colour-change points), and that step is [G1]'s gap already for maps without Condition 5 (configuration C5). |

- LEDGER.md:145 (row 100): | 100 | **The class theorem for character colourings (CLG), at existence scope: for every real number field `F` and `k ≥ 2`, given band rigidity (row 98), the plane has a band `k`-character — equivalently a character `k`-colouring `x ↦ ⌊kθ(x)⌋` — iff some stable place of `F(i)` carries one at some level of its residue tower; so `χ_char(F²) = min` over the residue targets, and at `k ≤ 5`, and at every `k` over fields of degree ≤ 2, a character `k`-colouring exists only if `L(F) ≤ k`** (FS-91; FS91-J07-1 to J07-15, J10-L1, J10-L2, J10-L7, J10-L8, J11-T12). **[hand (+ tools exact); two independent derivations (J07; J10 blind in phase 1, then from J07's 25 lemma statements: 24 TRUE, one GAP)]** The peel: by row 98, `θ = τ + α`; the archimedean part vanishes at every complex place and is an interval about 0 at each real one, so `τ` is band; `τ` factors through finitely many stable places (Lemma C: `R ⊗ ℤ_p = R̄_p × V_n`; Lemma C′: `R_F = O′`, the ring integral at the stable primes, with **no Galois hypothesis**, except possibly at a ramified stable dyadic place — three derivations; the exception occurs at ℚ(√5), ℚ(√17), ℚ(α)); weak approximation makes the image of `U` a product of local images; and a sum of symmetric subsets of the circle inside the band keeps **some** summand inside it (the peeling lemma, three derivations). CLG-g makes the exception explicit — a stable place carrying a band `k`-character only above level one, odd with residue field `𝔽_{p^f}`, `f ≥ 3`, `2p < k < χ(Γ_{p^f})`, or inert dyadic of norm `Q ≥ 64` — using level collapse for `k ≤ 2p` (four derivations) and every inert tower's `λ_min` scaling by `Q^{j−1}`; its corollary (g3) at `k = 7` holds only as "exact level `2e(𝔭\|3) + 1`" (a GAP in the lemmas, found by the verifier). **CLG is not a classification:** it does not say that every character colouring is pulled back from one place; that is proved at `k = 3` (row 99's no mixing) and at `k = 4` over ℚ(√59), ℚ(√83), ℚ(√131) (HD1, from CLG's *proof* with Lemma D — with no FS-66 certificate, no `[Zhang2012]`, no Lemma R), and open elsewhere (FS-92). Controls reproduced: `θ₂` at a ramified dyadic place; `[Fischer1994]`'s ℤ/4 colouring of K at one dyadic place, level 3 (one existing checker); C9 on ℚ(√11) at `Γ₇`; row 96's C-47 and C-167, C-167 with no certificate. With `A(R_F)` wide, FS89-CL3–CL5 are TRUE and FS89-CL2 false only as worded. **Not claimed:** anything about `χ` from four colours up (`Θ_k = ∅` is one-sided, G71); the classification at `k ≥ 4` beyond HD1's fields; whether CLG-g's exception is ever realised; a kernel proof. **Prior art:** the close gate found no statement of it; `χ_char` is `[BarajasSerra2008]`'s *regular chromatic number* (circulant graphs), `κ` the lonely-runner / view-obstruction parameter; Lemma C/C′ partly `[Fischer1990b]`'s (quadratic and multiquadratic cases), the general form not found, Shimura 1964 undecided; the peeling lemma not found (folklore-grade); `[Johnson2000]` and `[Johnson2006]` read 2026-09-27, not found; Lemma C′ answers `[Johnson2000]` §5's question at `n = 2` outside the exception fields (*"the problem of characterizing the additive group A generated by the unit vectors in 𝔽ⁿ"*, p. 177; `CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §2.7). | `plans/FS91-S1D-character.md`, `plans/FS91-S1V-character.md`, `data/fs91/j07/lemma-statements.md`, `plans/FS91-derivation.md` §2, §4, `tools/crunch_j07_checks.py`, `tools/crunch_j10_{checks,phase2}.py` | FS-91 |
- LEDGER.md:155 (row 110): | 110 | **Windows at five colours over ℚ(√167): no measurable window five-colouring reads a finite set of places with no real place and none above 43 (the dyadic place is split with ratio 0 — C14.2 (c) holds at `q = 2` —, the barrier prime's place is split, 59 has ratio `0.199119854095` exactly); every finite place except twelve (above 2, 3, 5, 7, 29, 61, split; above 23, 43, stable) is 5-thick, so a Borel window five-colouring over any `S` exists iff one exists over `S_red ⊆ {∞, 2, 3, 5, 7, 23, 29, 43, 61}`, and with a real place and only thick finite places the question is exactly `χ_B(ℝ²) ≤ 5`** (M3; `plans/M3-derivation.md` §5–§6). **[hand + tools exact + cert, two derivations (J21; J22 blind on the fixed list, from the statement on MX, TH, RED); 59 exact by two code bases; 23 by row 75's certificate]** So a window falsifier of the conjecture's "≥ 6" at ℚ(√167) must read a place above 43 (with no real place) or one of 2, 3, 5, 7, 23, 29, 43, 61 (with one) — or be a Borel (or null-set-tolerant) five-colouring of `ℝ²`. - LEDGER.md:159 (row 114): | 114 | **The colourings of a product with a gap-free factor. FS67(G, k) — every `k`-colouring of `G × H`, for every connected `H` with `χ(H) > k`, comes from `G` — holds iff every `k`-colouring of `G` is gap-free and `χ(E_k(G)) ≤ k`, `E_k(G)` the improper part of the exponential graph `K_k^G`; for `G = K_k × X` (connected, uniquely `k`-colourable) it fails iff `X` has a Hedetniemi partner, so at every `k ≥ 4` it fails for some finite connected uniquely `k`-colourable `G`** (FS-67; `plans/FS67-derivation.md` FS67-DA1 to DA4, L1, GL, CL, and for three colours DB1R, W, DB1Z, C5). **[hand, two blind derivations (J27D; J27V); at `k ≥ 4` from `[Tardif2023]` Thm 1]** The negative at `k ≥ 4` is **a composition of print** — Duffus–Sands–Woodrow's `B(n) ⟹ H(n)` (`[Tardif2008]` §4.2; `[GodsilEtAl2020]` §1.3) with `[Tardif2023]` — made explicit by `K_k × X`, which needs neither DSW nor `[Shitov2019]`. FS67 holds for `G = K_k` (Greenwell–Lovász, re-derived by a selection on `E_k(K_k)`; `K_k^s` by iteration, PA-P3) and for every `G` whose vertices all lie in `k`-cliques. **Three colours** (`B(3)` widened to gap-free): a two-step winding `D_C` is constant on components of `K_3^G`; components with `D_C ≡ 0` for some odd walk `C` are 3-colourable (`[TardifWrochna2019]` §3, external); the loopless components with every `D_C ≠ 0`, present with edges at `G = C₅` and 3-colourable there by hand, are the named GAP both minds reached. A gap-free colouring is a fall (frozen) colouring; Csernák's "tight" is that, not Hoffman-tight. **Not claimed:** three colours; any Hoffman-tight counterexample. **Prior art (`plans/FS67-PA-close.md` Stage 2):** `B(n) ⟹ H(n)` in print; whether DSW's proof uses `K_n × X` undecided (DSW unread); the characterisation and L1 not found as sentences. | `plans/FS67-J27D-derivation.md` §Stages 2, 4; `plans/FS67-J27V-verification.md` §2.1–§2.5, §4.2; `tools/crunch_j27d_da.py`, `tools/crunch_j27d_db.py` | FS-67 (crunch 2026-09-25) |
- LEDGER.md:163 (row 118): | 118 | **Rank one of Kronecker selection, and why Einsiedler–Lindenstrauss's classification does not apply. Over every real number field one character carries the selection (given row 98, a proper window forces a band character, hence `θ ∈ T + A`), and `θ ∈ E ∖ (T + A)` has `cl θ(U) = T`; at `F = ℚ` every `θ ∉ T + A` has `cl θ(U) = T`. No finitely generated `Γ ⊂ U_F` acts irreducibly on the solenoid `R̂` (`R = O′` inverts infinitely many primes, `ℤ[Γ]` finitely many), so EL2021 Thm 1.2 never applies on `R̂` itself; on the pieces `M̂` it applies exactly under admissibility (row 120)** (FS-100; FS100-N1, T4, T5, T2). **[hand; one mind each — J51V, J51D, J51D, J51]** For general `F` off `E` the rank-one density is a GAP at the field form of FS89's PA-B2 and JC over `R_{F,S}` (row 98's gluing does not transfer). The card's answer [reading, one mind] — MR as its zero-entropy half plus an owed PE-lim (from finitely generated `Γ` to `U`) — was right in shape; **both halves are now held (row 120)**: PE-lim through Thm 1.2 on the finitely generated pieces under admissibility, the zero-entropy half by Einsiedler–Fish; nothing known forces positive entropy from the four-colouring constraint. **Not claimed:** the rank-one density off `E` in general. **Prior art:** the nearest is the topological-rigidity line (`[Furstenberg1967]`, `[Berend1983]`, `[Wang2012]`, `[LindenstraussWang2012]`), whose hypotheses fail here (FS100-T2); T4 and T5 compose held lemmas. | `plans/FS100-derivation.md` FS100-N1, T4, T5, T2; `plans/FS100-three-field-lemma.md` §PA.3 | FS-100 (crunch 2026-09-26) |
- LEDGER.md:165 (row 120): | 120 | **Measure rigidity for the unit-vector group: for every real number field `F` with `R_F = O′` (row 100 — every `F` without a ramified stable dyadic place), every `U_F`-invariant finite positive measure on `R̂` with `τ(E) = 0` is a multiple of Haar measure (MR(F)). It splits by entropy: every `U`-invariant `τ` is `a·m + τ′` with `τ′` of zero entropy for every `u` (PE-2, through Einsiedler–Lindenstrauss's Thm 1.2 on the finitely generated pieces `M̂`, applied only under admissibility — every finite-index subgroup of `Γ` spans `L`); and one non-torsion `u` of zero entropy puts a `U`-invariant measure on `T + A` (EF_F⁺, Einsiedler–Fish's pigeonhole with the rational unit vectors as multipliers and the product formula as the Diophantine step). PE-1 as first posed is false (ℚ(√47); ℚ(√5)), and CE-1's hypothesis is false (a rotation circle), so MR was never the classical finitely generated question** (FS-100 stage 5, the ×2×3 crunch; `plans/FS100-derivation.md` FS100-EFP, EFZ, PE1, PE2, MR, CE1, EFG, EFX, HX1). **[hand; PE-2 two derivations blind to each other (X02D, X02V), applying EL2021 Thm 1.2 by statement — announced as EL2003 Thm 1.1, whose proof there is an outline (§6), it rests on EL2021 Thm 1.3 (external, published) and a deduction EL2021 never writes (FS105-DED, two minds, one blind); its hypotheses read on the pieces at ℚ(√635087) (FS105-R) and at de Grey's field (FS105-R; FS105-W, separately); EL2021 read at §§1, 2, 4 and the last step of Thm 4.1's proof in full, its cited leafwise-measure machinery unread — a reading gap, not a mathematical one; EF_F⁺ three derivations blind to one another (X01D, X12B; FS105-B at ℚ(√635087), with EF_F⁰) and a checking reading (X01V, after its blind GAP at the multiplier step, withdrawn for the premise FS105-B met blind); MR a composition, three minds; at degree 16 one checking walk (FS105-W)]** Mechanism: `U` has polynomially many elements of bounded height (`U_ℚ` alone gives `(4/π + o(1))M^{1/[F:ℚ]}` in `H_L`, and any positive exponent suffices — FS105-W-H, W-G), which is Einsiedler–Fish's hypothesis and which no finitely generated subgroup has (row 118's FS100-T2: none acts irreducibly either); the zero-entropy measures that survive sit on the archimedean isometric leaves, inside `E` (EF-X's circles). EF-G (a), `h_m(u) = log H(u)` with `H` over finite places, is false off the totally real fields (ℚ(∛2)); `h_m(u) = Σ_v log⁺\|u\|_v` over all places. HX-1: closed `U`-invariant subgroups of `R̂` are finite or everything. **Not claimed:** MR at row 100's exception fields (ℚ(√5), ℚ(√17), ℚ(α)), where the adelic model needs `R = O′`; anything about finitely generated `Γ` alone. **Prior art:** the zero-entropy method is `[EinsiedlerFish2010]` Lemmas 3.1–3.2 (on 𝕋; `[Sataev1975]` first, for the case of a semigroup holding the values of an integer polynomial — its abstract read); the positive-entropy input `[EinsiedlerLindenstrauss2022]` Thm 1.3, from which its Thm 1.2 follows (FS105-DED; announced as EL2003 Thm 1.1, whose §6 is an outline); the transfer to an `S`-adic solenoid or number field not found (four zbMATH queries; Huang 2017, Huang–Li–Shi–Xu 2023 read at §1: circle and tori only); EL2021 §§1, 2, 4 read in full (X24R; FS105-R): set-up for `ℤ^d` only, holding neither PE-2 nor EF_F⁺ (its Lemma 4.2 is a factor lift for a fixed `ℤ^d`); EF_F⁺ not in print (EF 2010's eleven citers in two databases, circle and tori only; Sataev 1975 is the circle theorem); PE-2's shape is Johnson's nonlacunary theorem (1992, `[JohnsonA1992]`, review only) transposed; MR not found under its discipline's names (five more zbMATH queries at FS-105; `[BekkaFrancini2022]` needs unipotent generation) — its nearest, `[LacaWarren2018]`'s "generalized Furstenberg conjecture" (extremal traces of `C*(J ⋊ O*)`), is the finitely generated case and open. | `plans/FS100-derivation.md` §3; `plans/FS100-X01D-derivation.md` §3–4; `plans/FS100-X12B-derivation.md` §2; `plans/FS100-X02D-derivation.md` §2–4; `plans/FS100-X02V-verification.md` §2; `plans/FS100-X01V-verification.md` §2, §4; `tools/x01d_unit_check.py`, `tools/x01v_entropy_check.py`, `tools/x02d_pe1_counterexample.py`, `tools/x02v_checks.py`, `tools/x12b_multiplier_count.py`; `plans/FS105-derivation.md` §1–§3, `tools/fs105_r_scope.py`, `tools/fs105_b_heights.py`, `tools/fs105_w_growth.py` | FS-100 stage 5 (crunch 2026-09-26); hardened at FS-105 |

- LEDGER.md:175 (row 130): | 130 | **The stationary number is a measurable one, and given MR so is Hadwiger–Nelson. With no MR: `χ_CP(ℝ²) = χ_m⁰(ℝ²) = χ(A_σ) ≤ χ_m(ℝ²) ≤ χ_B(ℝ²)`, where `χ_m⁰` is the least `k` with a Lebesgue-measurable `k`-colouring proper at almost every unit pair and `χ(A_σ)` is `[BachocEtAl2014]`'s operator chromatic number of the circle's convolution operator: a measurable colouring proper at almost every edge gives a CP one (the weak-* orbit closure in `L^∞(ℝ², Δ_k)`, continuity in probability free, properness surviving because the circle measure's Fourier transform decays, one random threshold) — for any Rajchman edge measure `σ` with `T ⊆ supp σ`, false without it. Given MR (row 128): `χ(ℝ²) = χ_m⁰(ℝ²)`, so `χ(ℝ²) ≥ 6` iff every measurable 5-colouring of the plane has a positive-measure set of monochromatic unit pairs (the "only if" half unconditional). Falconer's `χ_m` (proper at every edge) is one step further: `χ_m⁰ = χ_m` iff some a.e.-proper colouring's metrical boundary (`[Falconer1981]`'s null set, where every point sees two colours of positive density) is list-colourable from lists of size at least two — a question about finite configurations — and colourings with slack, which would give Borel ones, need six colours** (FS-103 Phase 3; FS103-M1, M1b, M1c, M-FREE, M-NR, M-NSUPP, M-AE3, M-EASY, M-D, M-Q4). **[hand; M1, M1b, M1c, M-FREE, M-AE3 and M-Q4 two derivations (FS103-M; FS103-N blind to M's record); `χ_m⁰ ≤ χ_CP` two blind (the verifiers); the null-set reduction, NR, N-SUPP and N-EASY one mind each]** **Not claimed:** `χ(ℝ²) = χ_m(ℝ²)` (the null-set step, a GAP in every record); `χ_B` against `χ_m`; any value. **Prior art:** not found in print as a question or either inequality (FS103-G §2.2); BDOV define `χ(A_σ)` and print `χ(A) ≤ χ_m`, not `χ(A_σ) = χ_m⁰`; the Rajchman step is in print for one class: `[Bukh2008]` Lemma 12 (support selection) and Cohen–Mani's Lemma 2.4 with its `ℓ∞` counterexample, Remark 2.5 (`[CohenMani2025]` §2.3, crediting Bourgain 1986 and Bukh); its `k`-class form not found (FS-111's gate). The argument of the unconditional `χ(ℝ²) ≤ χ_m⁰(ℝ²)` (N-EASY) is in print as `[GwynStavrianos2022]` Lemma 2, Theorem 2 and Corollary 1 (p. 12), stated for badness under an invariant mean on E(2) (infimum 0 suffices, every colouring, quantitative `1/|E(G)|`); N-EASY follows with Haar averages in place of the mean, a step not printed (two readers, the consolidation run's LW and LW2). The averaging device is `[Polymath16wikiProb]`'s (2018–19), whose Lemmas 1–40 give the easy direction (`χ(ℝ²) ≤ χ_CP(ℝ²)`) for a finitely additive invariant random colouring by union bounds; the hard direction (an a.e.-proper measurable colouring to a CP one) is in neither. The null-set reduction's compactness step, de Bruijn–Erdős for list colourings by Tychonoff, is `[Johnson1994choice]` p. 125 (LW3). **Naming:** `[KechrisMarks2020]`'s `χ⁰_µ` (invariant conull vertex sets) is not this `χ_m⁰` (null sets of edges). | `plans/FS103-derivation.md` §3; `plans/FS103-M-derivation.md`; `plans/FS103-N-verification.md`; `tools/fs103_m_controls.py`, `tools/fs103_n_checks.py` | FS-103 Phase 3 (2026-09-26) |
- LEDGER.md:176 (row 131): | 131 | **The measurable question is a stability question. With no MR and no Voronov, for every `k`: `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≤ k` ⟺ `χ_CP(ℝ²) ≤ k` ⟺ `µ_k⁰ = 1` ⟺ `lim_{ε→0} µ_k(ε) = 1` ⟺ for every `δ > 0` there are `ε > 0` and a periodic partial `k`-colouring by half-open grid squares with no monochromatic pair at any distance in `[1 − ε, 1 + ε]`, leaving density at most `δ` uncovered — `µ_k(ε)` the largest upper density of `k` disjoint measurable classes whose monochromatic pairs in that band are null, `µ_k⁰` the same with null unit pairs. At six, `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≥ 7` ⟺ `lim µ₆(ε) < 1`: `[SokolovVoronov2025]` §9's hypothesis, read uniformly in `ε`, is `χ_m⁰ = 7`, and a refutation needs no wild set — it is a sequence of periodic grid colourings with vanishing holes, the wildness only in the limit. The tight and slack suprema coincide (`µ₆⁰ = lim µ₆(ε) = lim µ₆^per(ε)`, `µ₆^per(ε) = µ₆(ε)`, all attained). The hole gap: `ε²/(6+ε)² ≤ 1 − µ₆(ε) ≤ δ₆ + O(ε)`, the lower bound given `[Voronov2023]` Thm 1.3, the upper from `[Parts2020c]`'s tiling (`δ₆ = 1.430172·10⁻⁴`); "`µ₆(ε) < 1` for every `ε`" ⟺ Thm 1.1. Two no-gos for proofs: an argument counting only holes met by unit circles or bicycles proves at most `1 − µ₆(ε) ≳ ε` (strips of width `2ε` at spacing 2 are effective holes meeting every circle), and a trichromatic-point argument cannot see partial colourings (erosion leaves every coloured point of chromaticity 1 at vanishing cost). It moves no value: every bound held vanishes with `ε`; its novelty over row 130 is the slack side and the periodic form** (FS-106; FS106-EQ, SUP, EPS2, HIT, PIX, REM, COMP, LOCAL, STAB). **[hand; EQ, SUP, EPS2 (given V-1.3), HIT, PIX, REM and COMP two derivations, the second blind (TQ; QB), EQ by a different route at its hard direction; its first equivalence is row 130's; "`µ₆(ε) < 1` ∀`ε` ⟺ V-1.1" one mind (QB); LOCAL over Parts [external]]** Mechanism: the circle measure is Rajchman (row 130's weak-* step), so a limit of near-colourings is a colouring after support selection; a stability proof must count a defect at an `ε`-independent scale — over positive measure, or at features of bounded-below scale (G82; row 133's cone lemma is such a certificate). **Not claimed:** any value; PD-EQ, the same equivalence counted in band pairs (A2, one mind, sketch); C-Q-LOCAL at `δ = δ₆` (GAP, likely TRUE). **Prior art:** not found in print as an equivalence (FS-106's gate; `[Voronov2023]`'s five citing works); S–V §9 prints the conditional and the order `S(ε) = Ω(ε²)`, unproved there; the periodic half of the compactness step is, for one class, `[DeCorteOliveiraVallentin2022]` Lemma 6.2 (FS-107's gate); the Rajchman step is in print for one class (`[Bukh2008]` Lemma 12; `[CohenMani2025]` Lemma 2.4 and Remark 2.5, crediting `[Bourgain1986]`); its `k`-class form not found. The step from a refutation to `χ(ℝ²) ≤ 6` is link 2's argument (`[GwynStavrianos2022]` Lemma 2 and Theorem 2, run with Haar averages; row 130), the holes filled with one colour, which leaves monochromatic unit pairs of density at most twice the holes'. `[Kainen1992]` p. 35 conjectures *"The chromatic number of the plane is robust."* (some band of positive width needs no more colours than the plane); given `[Voronov2023]` Thm 1.1 that is `χ(ℝ²) = 7`, and this row's question is its measurable form (the consolidation run's LW2, checked by FP). | `plans/FS106-derivation.md` §4; `plans/FS106-TQ-derivation.md` §§2–4; `plans/FS106-QB-verification.md` §2; `tools/fs106_qb_rem_constants.py` | FS-106 (2026-09-27) |

- LEDGER.md:178 (row 133): | 133 | **Stability at six meets at one seam. R's reduction: given the quantified cone lemma, `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≥ 7` (row 131's C-Q-STAB, disc form) follows from (A) a complementary pair of fat junctions at distance in `[η, 2 − η]`, blocking toward a common unit point, in every `U_R` of every band-proper partial six-colouring with small holes, and (B) a lower bound on their cone scale — for two such junctions force `|H ∩ B(w, µ/4)| ≥ (t₁ − 2r)(t₁′ − 2r′)`, `t₁ = s·sin(µ/2)/3`, for every `ε ≥ 0`, repair-robust and with an uncovered disc, when `s, s′ ≤ µη²/100`, `2r < t₁` and each colour's arc meets the arc of half-width `π/2 − µ` about the direction (FS107-R-CP). W's equivalence: at bounded arc complexity `K`, band-proper designs with vanishing holes exist iff a full tight a.e.-proper six-colouring by a map of complexity `≤ K` exists (⟸ at `O(K)`, `δ(ε) = O(ε)`, periodised by a seam strip), and in such a map complementary-type vertices within distance 2 are tangent pairs or bowties — the cone lemma read as a design rule (W-TAN), a GAP at cusp vertices. So stability at six has two routes, each needing a third statement (row 139): route M, the map NO (FS-110's question, then FS-114's) (FS-110; YES gives `χ(ℝ²) ≤ 6` with no MR, NO answers `[SokolovVoronov2025]` Question 2 a.e. for locally finite arc-bounded maps) with the map step; route R, (A) at every complexity with (B), the map NO idle. No-gos: polygonal designs of bounded complexity keep holes `≥ δ₀(K)`, bar `d = 2` (W-POLY); an argument reading at a flat swap only the away-facing colour is capped at `2εs(1 + o(1))`, while the facing colour alone certifies `s³/256` (OB′ corrected; SW). Measured: the hexagonal seven-colouring with one class absorbed by straight triangles stalls at hole density `π/(42√3) ≈ 0.0432`, attained by no member (HX-STALL). - LEDGER.md:185 (row 140): | 140 | **Route M's rungs: saturation pins interfaces between unit circles, maximal covers are free, and at five the map step is a reformulation. In a saturated colouring every two-colour interface point (H1, H2) lies on a unit arc whose centre carries both colours or on a `C^{1,1}` curve with `\|κ\| ≤ 1` between two tangent unit discs; (in, in) is impossible and no partner density is needed (FS111-M-PINCH, M-C11); on saturated maps without cusps there is no bend and no colour repeated at a vertex. De Grey's Reuleaux 4 + 3 seven-colouring (R7) is saturated in the strongest sense and a maximal cover; square and hexagonal nine-colourings are saturated. Every a.e.-proper colouring lies below a maximal cover (Zorn), whose classes are maximal unit-avoiding sets; a regular one is a fixed point of its own unit shadow, `int F = ℝ² ∖ (F + S¹)`, carved by its own unit circles, semiconcave, of finite perimeter (M2-FIX, M2-HULLC); a regular maximal partition is a measurable fall colouring and saturated, and selecting from a cover is a choice of whole components, blocked by a lens (DISC7). At five, a maximal cover with regular classes whose boundaries form a locally finite plane graph gives `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≥ 6` through the held "maps need six" (M2-REDUCE-C) — and that statement is equivalent to `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≥ 6` (VM2-EQ5): a reformulation, not a step toward six. At six, off a locally finite singular set, saturated interfaces have integer radii (M2-INTRAD), so the map step needs that set's local finiteness and no large-radius accumulation — FS-115's stuck ray. Universal forms fail: tight block structure at seven (DECO-B, two objects); arcs on covers (a constant-width seven-cover, DISCK7, in the corrected orientation). - LEDGER.md:186 (row 141): | 141 | **The finiteness rung: a maximal class is as wild as its bands' zero sets, and in a fall colouring a pinch climbs a short ladder toward a junction. A regular maximal unit-avoiding set can have infinitely many interior components in a bounded set (HC_K: unit triangles on a honeycomb of edge `2 + √3/3`, bands of length 1 vanishing on a Cantor set, discs of radius `1/2` at the face centres; exact in ℚ(√3), FS111-M3-HC), and with a fat Cantor set every maximal completion is irregular (M3-IRREG); a small move of one class removes the pinch (M3-MOVE). In a regular maximal partition the pinch passes to both neighbouring classes one unit away on the same set (M4-LADDER); the ladder of unit parallels is ordinary for at most `1/(2c)` rungs up and, given unit discs rolling from below, `≲ w²/(8h)` down (M4-CUT); a pinch-removing move runs along it with every class forced and no spare colour at any `k`, ending where the ladder stops — at a junction, a class with disconnected local parts, or an irregular class; whether it is absorbed there is open (GAP; FS-120). It moves no value** (FS-111; FS111-M3-VAC, M3-HC, M3-FIN-U, M3-D3, M3-MOVE, M3-COVER, M3-CASCADE, M3-IRREG, M3-FIXCONV, M3-BAND, M4-LADDER, M4-CUT, M4-HC, M4-MOVE; MUA-FIN-L GAP). **[hand + tools exact; one mind each (M3; M4), no verifier; M4-CUT's downward half conditional]** Mechanism: a flat side facing its own class across 2 needs material at distance exactly 1 — a band whose thickness maximality does not fix. **Not claimed:** a partition with a pinched class (none built); MUA-FIN-L; that every maximal cover can be made regular. **Prior art:** fall colourings (Dunbar et al. 2000, finite graphs); closing and opening by discs (Matheron, Serra; unread); the objects and the ladder not found in print. | `plans/FS111-derivation.md` (rounds 3–4; the named gaps); `plans/FS111-M3-finiteness.md`; `plans/FS111-M4-cascade.md`; `tools/fs111_m3_honey.py`, `tools/fs111_m4_rungs.py` | FS-111 (2026-09-28) |
- LEDGER.md:187 (row 142): | 142 | **The honest class's ladders end inward and never outward. In an honest saturated colouring with REG-J′, at every number of colours, a curved interface's ladder of unit parallels ends inward at a unit arc whose centre carries its outer colour — a (Q) arc, or an (R2) arc about a point of Σ of at least three colours — and outward it never ends: a tangent arc of radius `n` forces tangent arcs of radii `n + 1, n + 2, …` over one window; with finitely many radii, every doubly periodic colouring among them, every curved interface is a split (Q) arc; LRA at a point is LRA along a whole unit-spaced normal line. At a point of Σ no colour holds two sectors, and a colour with a sector holds nothing else there; a tangent edge's partner curve carries its colour pattern pointwise, so tile edges at a convex corner are short. No three-colour (R2) end is built or excluded, and no curved tangent interface is held at any `k`. It moves no value** (FS-121; FS121-O1-ENDS, -OUT, -PER, -LRA-LINE, FS121-DB1-L3, FS121-V2-SECTOR, FS121-D1-VT, -EROSION; C121-R2-FOUR, C121-CURVED GAP). **[hand; eight statements two minds, the second blind (one prior); the rest one mind]** Mechanism: a ladder is a parallel family of rolling-ball curves, and it ends at its focal point. **Not claimed:** that any ladder ends at a (Q) arc only; an (R2) end, or a curved tangent interface, in any colouring. **Prior art:** FS-112's arc kinds and row 135's pencil, for valid maps; saturated colourings not found in print. | `plans/FS121-derivation.md` (round 1); `plans/FS121-map-step.md` § 3; `plans/FS121-DB1-verification.md`; `plans/FS121-D1-proof.md`; `plans/FS121-V2-verification.md`; `plans/FS121-V3-verification.md` | FS-121 (2026-10-03) |
- LEDGER.md:188 (row 143): | 143 | **Honesty is free, and the map step's six-colour content is the top colour. Every a.e.-proper `k`-colouring becomes, without raising any point's colour index, a measurable Grundy colouring bound at every interface on every colour present but the largest; every measurable idomatic partition (fall colouring) is honest saturated, and saturation is strictly weaker (a circle model, exact). So honest saturation at six is binding the top colour (TOP₆), and given a NO for honest saturated six-colourings it is the answer restated. On the cover route a Grundy six-colouring that is a map lies in `𝒫_6`, and `𝒫_6 = ∅` (NO-PAR) contains the honest route's saturated segment-and-arc NO; the stub's NO-PAR read on a maximal cover's map is the wrong target (DISC7's merged map, exact over all 5040 orders), and row 134's N4 step does not transfer to smooth boundaries (flat horns, exact). It moves no value** (FS-121; FS121-D3-GRUNDY, -FALLSAT, -SWAP1D, -EQ, FS121-C1-BOUND, -GMAP, -SAT, -COVMAP, -FLAT; C121-SAT6, C121-NOPAR, C121-NOPAR-C5 GAP). **[hand + tools exact; seven statements two minds, the second blind (one prior); the rest one mind]** Mechanism: the finite Grundy argument, made measurable; a map is bound one-sidedly by its lower colours. **Not claimed:** TOP₆; G-FIN₆; NO-PAR; that the cover route avoids FS-116's NO. **Prior art:** Grundy and fall colourings of finite graphs (Dunbar et al. 2000); measurable forms not found in print. | `plans/FS121-derivation.md` (rounds 2–3); `plans/FS121-D3-proof.md`; `plans/FS121-C1-proof.md`; `plans/FS121-V3-verification.md`; `plans/FS121-V4-verification.md`; `tools/fs121_d3_swap1d.py`, `tools/fs121_c1_disc7.py`, `tools/fs121_c1_flat.py` | FS-121 (2026-10-03) |
- LEDGER.md:189 (row 144): | 144 | **Symmetric and grid colourings are regular; what is open is aperiodic. No honest saturated REG-J′ colouring invariant under a cocompact group of isometries, so none doubly periodic, has large-radius accumulation, at any `k`; LRA forces thin pencils, maximal radius-`r` arcs with windows tending to 0 at every `r ≥ 2`. A same-colour piece of lateral half-width `ρ` facing a flat side across a gap `g` with `2g < ρ²` leaves the side's point no contact (sharp at `ρ² ≥ 2g − g²`), so every honest saturated grid colouring with countable breakpoints has a locally finite singular set; on the line the analogue is false with two colours, regions can be as small as wished at sixteen colours, and a fall colouring with a Cantor pinch would refute local finiteness. Three open claims — a three-colour (R2) end, a curved tangent interface, LRA — fall together to one: curved tangent interfaces in aperiodic colourings. It moves no value** (FS-121; FS121-D2-SYM, -THIN, -PERFIN, FS121-V5-SYM-CPT, FS121-D4-FACE, -GRID, -LINE, -TINY16, -HOLE, -FALL, FS121-Z1-NOCURVED; C121-NOLRA, C121-REGJ GAP). **[hand + tools exact; three statements two minds, the second blind (one prior), GRID's given FACE by name; the rest one mind]** Mechanism: counting modulo congruence bounds windows; the unit circle's sagitta against a gap. **Not claimed:** ¬LRA or REG-J′ in general; that a tight colouring can be taken periodic. **Prior art:** totally separable packings (`[FejesToth1973]`, listing only); measurable versions not found. | `plans/FS121-derivation.md` (rounds 2, 4; the close's composition); `plans/FS121-D2-proof.md`; `plans/FS121-D4-proof.md`; `plans/FS121-V5-verification.md`; `tools/fs121_d4_tiny16.py`, `tools/fs121_v5_face.py` | FS-121 (2026-10-03) |
- LEDGER.md:190 (row 145): | 145 | **The local exclusion decides both ways, and the unit circle about a four-colour vertex is a twisted circle colouring. For every `k`, valid `k`-coloured fragments — segment-and-arc maps of discs with no two interior points of one colour at distance exactly 1 — on discs of every radius give `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≤ k`, with no map, centre or complexity bound in the limit; so at six they give `χ(ℝ²) ≤ 6` with no MR, and `χ_m⁰(ℝ²) ≥ 7` forces a radius at which no disc carries a valid six-coloured fragment. In a six-coloured fragment, off the boundary the unit circle about a vertex of at least four colours carries the two colours absent at it, alternating under the 60° rotation, and every change point whose `±60°` germs lie off (Q) arcs is itself a vertex of at least four colours; in a saturated fragment every edge at such a vertex is a unit arc about a ring point carrying both its side colours, or a tangent segment or arc of radius at least 1. With (Q) arcs on the ring the ring-scale form fails: the disk's corner holds a two-colour change point below radius `≈ 1.5985`. - LEDGER.md:192 (row 147): | 147 | **In non-Siamese simple valid six-coloured fragments, vertices of at least four colours propagate along the unit circle: at a vertex `p` of four colours every semi-hub of its ring — a change point with a germ on a (Q) arc — places a vertex of at least four colours at itself or 60° from it along the ring, but for a full parallel semi-hub of three colours; paired (Q) germs of one colour pair swap inner and outer colour; at a hub the star of `p` fixes the hub's colours and missing pair, and antipodal hubs exchange tip and outer colour. [hand; GPT Astra; conditional on G4 and T7]** For every real multiquadratic `F ⊇ K` with `L(F) ≤ 5`: `χ(F²) = L(F)`. *Proof:* `local-global-further-results.md` §6. `L(F) = 4`: the Moser spindle gives `χ(F²) ≥ 4`; a multiquadratic dyadic completion has residue degree at most 2; ramified is excluded by the unit triangle (it would give a bipartite upper bound against the triangle); residue degree 2 would put `√−3` into the completion, and with `√3 ∈ F` that gives `i`, making the prime split (contradicting "stable, residue degree 2"); so any stable dyadic prime has residue field `𝔽₂` with unramified quadratic residue extension `𝔽₄`, graph `K₄` — the upper bound matches the spindle's lower bound. `L(F) = 5`: no stable dyadic prime exists (else `L(F) ≤ 4`), so the wall is broken; T7 gives `χ(F²) ≥ 5`; the residue bound at the field's best usable prime (11, in every case this covers) gives `χ(F²) ≤ 5`. Read here, sound and conditional (`VERIFICATION.md` row 7). *Proof verdict:* closes, conditional on `G₄` (same status as T7). *Rests on it:* the conjecture's proved range (G48, item 10 of "Where the problem stands"); FS-46's `ℚ(√3,√5)` test case (the smallest field with `√3 ∈ F` and `L=5` open, since T4's product route cannot reach four colours and T8 needs the wall-broken half, which `ℚ(√3,√5)` also satisfies — but `G₄`'s conditionality is the same gap FS-46 §1 already names). *Prior art:* ours. *Kernel:* stub (FS-47, D6 — the completion-degree arithmetic is named there as FS-37's territory unless it falls out of D2's `𝒪`-as-data form).

- plans/FS106-TS-derivation.md:273: | **C-T-IND** | **MISSTATED; corrected form TRUE** | 1 / [hand] | **As stated it cannot hold in general:** an integer index read from density classes along a curve is the lift to `K₃`'s universal cover, and it exists only when the trace changes colour finitely often on compact pieces; K3's classes give traces with infinitely many changes along **every** curve crossing the fat edge (each gap of the Cantor set is an `A`-interval, the Cantor set itself a.e. `B`), and nearby curves are no better, so no approximation defines one. So "no index exists" is proved for traces of that shape. **Corrected statement:** for two curves of C-T-ANN's kind with complementary parametrisation whose density traces have finitely many essential colour changes, `Ind` (the lift difference) satisfies `|Ind γ₁ − Ind γ₂| ≤ 1` whenever C-T-L1 holds at the simultaneous changes. Proof: FS106-V's repaired Prop. 12 (the state machine on colour pairs; the swap is exactly a bichromatic unit pair of one multicolour, excluded by C-T-L1; the other simultaneous changes decompose). So the index needs a **finiteness hypothesis on the traces** — the substitute §IQ asked for is "finitely many essential changes", and whether a tight 6-colouring can be forced to have it near a trichromatic point is the question K3 poses to FS-107. |

- plans/FS61-derivation.md:15 (after entry FS61-T1): **FS61-T1 — Eight new certified lower bounds for real quadratic planes, five of them exact values. [tools; the FS-61 implementer, using FS-58's frozen checker and generator (`tools/fs58_check_witness.py`, `tools/fs58_search.py`)]** For `d ∈ {59, 119, 239, 359, 431}`, `χ(ℚ(√d)²) = 4` (the lower bound is this milestone's; the upper bound `≤ 4` is prior art — the dyadic bound `[Fischer1990]` Thm 10(i) at `d = 59` (`d ≡ 3 mod 8`), the residue plane at 7, `[Moorhouse2010]` Thm 8.1, at `d = 119, 239, 359, 431` (`d ≡ 7 mod 8`, `L = 4`)); for `d ∈ {143, 311, 335}`, `4 ≤ χ(ℚ(√d)²) ≤ 5` (the lower bound is this milestone's; the upper bound `≤ 5` is prior art via the residue plane at 11, since `L(ℚ(√d)) = 5` at these three). Each lower bound is witnessed by a finite unit-distance graph (`data/fs61/witness-<d>/witness.json`, sizes 456–2,360 points, 1,122–6,639 edges) certified `CERTIFIED` by a standard-library-only checker sharing no code with the generator, which (i) re-derives every unit-distance edge from the coordinates in exact integer arithmetic and refuses unless it equals the file's edge list, (ii) checks a pinned edge is real, (iii) rebuilds the CNF from its own edge set and replays an LRAT refutation of 3-colourability, (iv) is refused when asked about the wrong radicand, (v) passes twelve planted-fault controls each refused for its own named reason, (vi) is independently re-derived in exact `Fraction` arithmetic against three solvers outside the generator's own, and (vii) carries an explicit proper 4-colouring verified on every edge (so each graph is exactly 4-chromatic). A ninth object, a 189-point witness at `d = 11` found by the same direct route, is also `CERTIFIED`; it does not change a value (`χ(ℚ(√11)²) = 4` is already kernel-held, `four_le_chromaticNumber_planeOver_eleven`, `Proofs/HadwigerNelson/Instances/SqrtEleven.lean`) but is a genuinely different graph (204-point chain witness in the kernel; this 189-point one from a single lattice ball) — it is the first certificate that `χ(ℚ(√11)²) ≥ 4` transfers to the **direct** route, contradicting the reading that the direct route cannot reach `d ≡ 3 (mod 8)` fields (see FS61-T3's neighbourhood). Four more fields (`23, 47, 71, 95`) were re-certified from new, often smaller, balls; their **values** are prior art within this project (`[FS58-T8]`, `plans/FS58-derivation.md`), so they are not refiled here — only the new witness objects are new. *Proof:* `data/fs61/census.md` (the table); `data/fs61/records/phase-C.md`, `phase-F.md`, `phase-F-239.md` (one row per gate item per field, every plant, every exit status); `data/fs61/witness-{59,119,143,239,311,335,359,431}/{certified,controls,wrong-radicand,recheck}.out` and `colouring4.json`; `data/fs61/owner/recheck-all.out` (the product owner's own decorrelated recheck, a second implementation, sha256 per witness, `RECHECK PASS` on all). *Proof verdict:* closes. *Rests on it:* `LEDGER.md` row 74 (item 1); `data/fs61/aiming-167.md` (these are the census population FS-59 draws its comparison from); FS-60 (`plans/STUB-quadratic-witnesses-kernel.md` §"What FS-61 added"), which names this exact field list as its input set. *Prior art:* ours (the witnesses); the method (saturate a lattice → filter by finite quotient → refuse a ball → extract → certify) is FS-58's, already attributed there; the upper bounds are `[Fischer1990]` Thm 10(i) and `[Moorhouse2010]` Thm 8.1, both already correctly cited in every witness `README.md` and in `frame.jsonl`. zbMATH: `ab:"unit distance graph" & ab:"quadratic field" & ab:"chromatic"` (0 — already run at FS-61's own opening, §PA-R, reproduced here), `ti:"chromatic number" & ab:"real quadratic"` (0). *Kernel:* stub (FS-60) — the certificate type over `Adjoin d ℤ`, parametric in `d`, that FS-60 designs takes exactly this witness format; the upper halves' kernel form is row 57's hand gap (FS-37/FS-47), also unclosed.

- plans/FS67-derivation.md:160 (after entry FS67-DB2): **FS67-DB2 — DB-2 (= J27D #16, J27V-T6): "`UD(𝔽₉, 2)`, and every level of the 3-adic tower that is Hoffman-tight at three colours, is connected and 3-gap-free." Level one: the circle has 8 points spanning `𝔽₉²`, spectrum `{8, 5⁸, 2²⁴, −1³², −4¹⁶}` (`λ_min = −4 = −d/2`), 3-coloured by a linear form — J27D's `x₀ + y₀ + x₁`, J27V's derived `Tr((1−i)u + iv)`. Every level `(ℤ₃[i]/3^j)²` is connected (Nakayama / Frattini) and 3-colourable (reduction); tightness per level is the hypothesis, not proved. [hand plus tools exact; two blind derivations with different colourings]**
- plans/FS67-derivation.md:180 (after entry FS67-DD2N): **FS67-DD2N — where a tight counterexample cannot live (= J27D #19; J27V agrees, §2.10 and §4.2): for every Hoffman-tight `k`-colourable `G` (tight ⟹ gap-free, FS67-T), no `H = Cay(A, T)` with `λ* < 1/(k−1)` witnesses the failure of FS67(G, k) (FS67-DC2); for 1-walk-regular tight `G`, every witness `H` has `χv(H) ≤ k < χ(H)` (FS67-DC1). So a tight counterexample, if one exists, lives in the gap regime of `H`, where Hedetniemi counterexamples live (one factor of a pair has `χ_f ≤ k`, `[Zhu2011]`). [hand; J27D; the 1-walk-regular half derived independently by J27V §2.10, the abelian-Cayley half checked by J27V §4.2]**

- plans/M63H2-derivation.md:17 (after entry M63H2-T3): **M63H2-T3 — Sealing depends on 11's residue degree alone; a ramified prime seals exactly as a split one does. [hand; the product owner, 2026-09-12, closing a question the advisor raised]** For `F = K(√d)`, `K = ℚ(√3,√11)`, with `11` **ramified** in `F/K` (`e=2, f=1`): the residue field at 11 is `𝔽₁₁`, `q ≡ 3 (mod 4)`, `i` is absent from it, the prime of `F(i)` above 11 stays conjugation-fixed, and `CLAUDE.md` G43's bound `χ(F²) ≤ χ(UD(𝔽₁₁,2)) = 5` applies — the field is **5-sealed**, exactly as when 11 splits (`e=1,f=1`, same residue field `𝔽₁₁`). Only when 11 is **inert** (`e=1,f=2`, residue field `𝔽₁₂₁`, `q ≡ 1 (mod 4)`) does `i` enter the residue field, the prime of `F(i)` split, no bound survive, and the field become 6-open. *Proof:* the three-way `(e,f)` case table (split/ramified/inert → residue field → `q mod 4` → is `i` present → usable?); and ramification cannot otherwise enter the argument because the reduction never needs points integral, only differences, and a unit-vector difference has valuation 0 whatever `e` is. `plans/M63-phaseH2-the-family.md` §5. *Proof verdict:* closes. *Rests on it:* the screen's gate D at `n=36` (radicand `143=11·13`, 11 ramified there) and every family member where `11 |` the squarefree part of `4n−1`; refines `CLAUDE.md` G43's "6-open ⟺ wall broken ∧ 11 inert" to "∧ 11 has residue degree 2". *Prior art:* the surrounding machinery (residue field, conjugation-fixedness, the finite-plane bound) is this project's own kernel result (ledger row 52); the ramified case had been an open guess in this project (`d % 11 ≠ 0` as an untested convention) until this derivation closed it; nearest held: `[Moorhouse2010]`; zbMATH: `ab:"unit distance graph" & ab:"finite field"` (1 hit, unrelated — a Zarankiewicz-problem paper), `ab:"residue field" & ab:"chromatic number"` (2 hits, unrelated). *Kernel:* declined — an instance of G43's ring-level theorem (row 52, kernel); the field-level step is row 57's known gap, FS-47's territory.
- plans/M63H2-derivation.md:25 (after entry M63H2-T7): **M63H2-T7 — The counting compulsion (`CLAUDE.md` G53): a base-alone floor that can refute a type-M arena. [derived; the product owner, from a solver-agent measurement, 2026-09-12]** For a type-M arena (base `B` plus a rotated copy sharing the centre `0`, joined by the diagonal cross edges of the free circle) and any colour count `k`: a proper `k`-colouring `C` of the arena gives two proper `k`-colourings of `B` alone, `f(v)=C(v)` and `g(v)=C(Rv)` — `R0=0` gives `f(0)=g(0)`, and each diagonal cross edge `(v,Rv)` gives `f(v)≠g(v)`. With `B(h) = \{$circle $v : h(v)=h(0)\}$}`, any `v ∈ B(f)∩B(g)` would give `f(v)=f(0)=g(0)=g(v)`, contradicting the second fact — so `B(f)` and `B(g)` are disjoint. Hence, with `L = min` over proper `k`-colourings of `B` of `|B(h)|`: **`2L > (\text{circle count}) ⟹ $the arena is not $k$-colourable}`**, a property of the base alone, no arena solve. *Proof:* `plans/M63-phaseH2-the-family.md` §6e, three steps. Uses only the diagonal cross edges (off-diagonal ones can only help the refutation); sufficient, not necessary. **Instantiated on `ρ₆₃`'s base at `k=4`:** `L=10` exactly (M63H2-T10's exhaustive enumeration: `≤9` UNSAT, `≤10` SAT, two-sided), circle count 30, so `2L=20 ≤ 30` — the criterion is **silent** on `ρ₆₃`; it does not itself refute, and `ρ₆₃`'s non-4-colourability rests on the separate argument of M63H2-T11. *Proof verdict:* closes (the general theorem is sound; its non-firing on `ρ₆₃` is a correctly reported negative result, not a gap). *Rests on it:* ledger row 64's mention of the family; `CLAUDE.md` G53 in full. **Not a theorem, demoted here:** the earlier reading "exactly 10 of 30, every time" from twelve *reseeded* colourings — retracted in the same source once pattern-blocking (which forbids, rather than merely re-asks for, a different colouring) exhibited a colouring with all 30 circle points at the centre's colour; the "provisional 10/30" is a floor (`L ≥ 10`) established by a flawed sampling method, not an invariant, and is superseded by M63H2-T10's exact `L=10`. *Prior art:* ours; the shape (a lower-bound "colour-class floor" argument from an isometric pair of colourings glued at one shared point) has no located citation; nearest held: none specific; zbMATH: `ab:"unit distance graph" & ab:"chromatic number" & ab:"rotated copy"` (0 hits), `ab:"coincidence site lattice" & ab:"coloring"` (0 hits). *Kernel:* declined — a short counting argument over `Fin k`-colourings of a finite graph; a natural small Lean statement (any `k`, any type-M arena) if the construction is ever formalised, worth a stub beside `Forcer.lean`.

rational band rigidity at two or more primes, at every band, and its field consequences (rows 95–97, 98); the class theorem for character colourings, the character chromatic numbers, three colours over every real number field and one prime's three failures (rows 99–102); as far as the gate reached, Duffus–Sands–Woodrow 1985 unread); prime inversion in valuation form, with no number field (row 105); four-colour rigidity split into invariant measures and transience, Theorem C over the real S2 fields and its ℚ(√47) form (rows 111–113); Kronecker selection refuted as stated, its reduction and its corrected form's reduction to one density step (row 116); T-C(A)'s class among real quadratic fields and SP-Cor over it (row 117); rank one of the selection and the non-irreducibility of the unit-vector group on its solenoid (row 118); the winding form of the three-colour criterion's ⟹ as a `Θ₃` certificate's semantics, and the certificate's kernel checker (row 119; as far as the gate reached; its ingredients are in print); the entropy split of measure rigidity for the unit-vector group and its Einsiedler–Fish transfer, and `χ(ℚ(√47)²) = 5` (rows 120–122); the class theorem and SP-Cor in every degree, so the local–global conjecture's main clause through five over every real number field, `χ(ℚ(√3,√5)²) = 5`, the type-(B) fields' rotation-invariant half and the reduction of six at ℚ(√167) to `χ_CP(ℝ²) ≥ 6` (rows 123–125) — not found in print (X24R; - LEDGER.md:110 (row 66): | 66 | **The fan gap, and the arity-2 compulsion structure over sealed fields** (FS-38). A colour absent from a vertex's closed neighbourhood in some proper colouring of a target graph (`FanGap C v γ`), repainted at any vertex above it along a graph homomorphism, frees that vertex from every forced pair: `not_forces_of_fanGap` (no forces-freeness or colourability premise), `not_fan_of_fanGap` (a fan survives only if some partner's image sits on the gap colour in every gapped colouring), `not_separated_of_sharedGap` (one colour absent from both closed neighbourhoods, repainted at a non-adjacent pair); `Slack.forces_iff` (`Forces ↔ =`), `PairwiseFusable.separated_iff_adj` (`Separated ↔ Adj`) and their `_of_hom` forms; the transports `FanGap.comap`, `Slack.of_hom` (no surjectivity), `slack_addCayley_of_fanGap` (one gapped vertex is slackness everywhere on a Cayley graph), `unitQuadrance_fusable_of_sharedGap_base` (one shared-gap table at the base edge, by translation and `rotHom`), `Slack.unitQuadrance_of_ringHom`; `exists_fanGap_iff_not_coverAtLeast`; `three_le_arityFloor_of_forces_eq_of_separated_adj`. **Instances:** `Γ₁₁` is slack and pairwise fusable at five colours — the certified table's gap at `(2,4)` and a second 121-entry table with colour 4 absent from `N[(0,0)] ∪ N[(1,0)]`, decided — so for every graph with a homomorphism into it **`Forces G 5 u u' ↔ u = u'` and `Separated G 5 u u' ↔ G.Adj u u'`** (`forces_iff_of_hom_finitePlane11`, `separated_iff_adj_of_hom_finitePlane11`): `ZMod (11^m)`, `ℤ_[11]`, `Adjoin 11 ℤ_[11]`, every `Valid` √7-free certificate with `11 ∤ D` (`UDCert.forces_eq_11`, `UDCert.separated_iff_adj_11` — every pair, no distance hypothesis), and **Heule-510 unconditionally** (`heule510_forces_iff`, `heule510_separated_iff_adj`, `three_le_arityFloor_heule510` — G31's measured null promoted, the pair `(0, 475)` M60 was silent on included). At 19 the forces half on the kernel's own periodic colouring (`finitePlane19_slack`; gapped classes `y − x ∈ {4, 10}`). **Consequences:** over every 5-sealed multiquadratic field ⊇ K — each has 11 usable of residue degree one — a five-colour forced pair is a single vertex and a five-colour virtual edge a unit edge, at every distance: G48's six-level question is answered **no** (arity 2), FS-6 has no instance, FS-16's 51 candidates are none of them virtual edges, and row 55's distance law is the level-one shadow of this. **Not claimed:** the field-level hand step of M58/M60 (row 57's gap); anything at arity ≥ 3; anything over a 6-open field, where no 5-colourable residue target exists. **Prior art:** a gapped vertex is the reconfiguration literature's non-frozen vertex (`[BrewsterEtAl2020]`, folklore); the transport along a homomorphism and its compulsion consumers were not found (plan §PA). | `FanGap.lean`, `Instances/FanGap11.lean`, `Instances/FanGap19.lean`, `data/fs38/` | FS-38 |
- LEDGER.md:111 (row 67): | 67 | **The 2-adic tower stabilises at level 4** (FS-38). `P_m = Cay(O/𝔓^m, S_m)`, `O = ℤ₂[ω][√3]`, `𝔓² = (2)` — the local plane of K(i) above 2 at **four** colours, in a ring-free representation (`twoAdicPlane m`, an `addCayley` under two Eisenstein forms; that it is `Cay(O/𝔓^m, S_m)` is [hand], three independent constructions agreeing). **Kernel:** level 4 is pairwise free (`twoAdicFour_forces_iff`, five tables through the connection set); level 5 forces exactly the kernel of the level map — `Forces (twoAdicPlane 5) 4 0 d ↔ twoAdicLevelMap 4 d = 0` (`twoAdicFive_forces_iff`), the backward half from **one LRAT refusal replayed by the kernel** on the refutation's own 125-vertex, 778-edge core (`twoAdicFive_not_colorable_with_edge`; the pins' clique proved from the edge list) and the `ω`-orbit, the forward half by pullback; no level ≤ 5 has a fan gap (`twoAdicFive_not_slack`, `twoAdicFour_not_slack`, `twoAdicPlane_one_no_fanGap`). **Derived** [hand, `plans/FS38-derivation.md` D-2, Theorems D2-A/B]: every `P_m` is Hoffman-tight at four colours (`λ_min = −d/3`; by FS38-L2's non-strict half and the exact table — FS38-L1's tightness half does not follow, and "tightness propagates up a constant-fibre tower" is false in general, row 93), so every 4-colouring is balanced, gap-free and periodic under `H_min`, the common kernel of the minimising characters; the minimisers stabilise at level 4 (45 characters, trivial common kernel), so for every `m ≥ 5` **every 4-colouring of `P_m` is pulled back from `P₄`** — the tower's colouring space is `P₄`'s 1,179,936 colourings — with `Forces ⟺ x − y ∈ 𝔓⁴` and `Separated ⟺ y − x ∈ S_m + 𝔓⁴`; the depth 4 has an arithmetic origin, a lattice certificate: four norm-one units `≡ 1 (mod 𝔓)` whose `t − 1` span has 2-adic elementary divisors 1, 2, 4, 4, so `𝔓⁴ ⊆ M` and `𝔓³ ⊄ M`. [tools] Measured at levels 1–8 under controls (the forced set exactly `{depth ≥ 4}`, two engines, verified colourings; the level-9 spectrum met out of sample); a 3-adic control met with no solver (every 3-colouring of `UD(ℤ/9,2)` is 3-periodic); the 11-adic tower slack at every level [kernel, row 66]. **The classifier:** the sign of `λ_min + d/(k−1)` of the level-one plane — tight at 2 (k = 4) and 3 (k = 3), slack at 7, 11, 19, 23, 83 — and by the Weil bound no usable prime above 3 in the committed table is tight for any `k ≤ 7`. **The transfer is freeness only:** a K-fabric with a homomorphism into `P₄` cannot force a four-colour pair of depth ≤ 3 (the mechanism behind M53's zeros at depth 2), while the tower's forcing is an extremal property of the whole Cayley graph and says nothing about any finite chunk — M56's depth-6 fact stays unexplained, and P-D3 registers that no finite K²-graph forces depth 4. [hand 2], that a K-certificate draws into `P_m`, is row 57's gap, unchanged. **Prior art:** the ratio bound and its equality case (`[Hoffman1970]`, unpublished by him per `[Haemers2021]`; `[Delsarte1973]`, `[Lovasz1979]`, `[GodsilMeagher2016]`); "k-tight" is the literature's *Hoffman colorable*, and balance plus gap-freedom is Blokhuis–Brouwer–Haemers 2007 Prop. 2.3 (`[AbiadEtAl2025]`); `[MMST1998]` holds the spectra over `ℤ/p^r`; the Fourier periodicity and the classifier were not found. | `TwoAdicTower.lean`, `Instances/TwoAdicFour.lean`, `Instances/TwoAdicFive.lean`, `Instances/TwoAdicFive/`, `plans/FS38-derivation.md`, `plans/FS38-tower-records.md`, `data/fs38/` | FS-38 |

- LEDGER.md:123 (row 78): | 78 | **Four colours on de Grey's lattice: `F = P = 8Λ` — every four-colouring of it is `8Λ`-periodic — and where else a lattice can be rigid** (FS-73; the theorems `plans/FS73-derivation.md` T1–T20, T31–T38, `LEDGER.md` § B; the understanding is G69, G71). **The question** (FS-73's C3-inherit, in subgroup form): for a lattice `Λ` of unit vectors, the *forced group* `F` (differences every four-colouring of `Cay(Λ, U)` identifies) against the *H1 group* `P` (differences every **periodic** colouring identifies), `F ⊆ P`; independently by Glucose DRUP + drat-trim on a 39,829-point ball with live negative controls, and by Lingeling with no symmetry break); `v`'s orbit under the order-24 point group spans `8Λ` (index 4,096), so `F ⊇ 8Λ`; three exhibited `8Λ`-periodic separators give `P ⊆ 8Λ`; hence **`F = P = 8Λ`** (**derived at FS-85 with no certificate, from Parts's trees: row 85**), C3-inherit holds at K, and the lattice's four-colourings are exactly those of `Cay(Λ/8Λ, U mod 8)`: **380 up to the pinned colour permutation (9,120 in all), 12 classes**. `8/9 = −(v + v̄)` and Harder's `8/3` are elements of `F`; colourings at period `2^a·m` are `2^min(a,3)`-periodic, the odd periods 15 and 21 have none, and `P_32 = 8Λ` (four UNSAT confirmations, registered before the run) is a consistency check that could have failed. **G70 held on every four-colouring:** the only √3-clean ones are Theorem B's two residue colourings, one orbit. **P-D3 (row 67) is refuted** by derivation: `G ∪ (G + 8/3)` forces `16/3`, 2-adic depth 4 at both primes (T3). **[tools] The classification table** (32 lattices, `data/fs73/table/TABLE.md`, corrections in `CORRECTIONS.md`): at four colours de Grey's is the only rigid rank-4 CM lattice; every CM subfield of ℚ(√3,√5), ℚ(√3,√23), ℚ(√3,√47) and K's other subfields is loose by a gapped periodic colouring at `N ∈ {2, 3, 5, 7}`; **[hand] the fixed-prime gap lemma** (a conjugation-fixed prime with a gapped target makes every lattice of that CM field loose) reaches no lattice spanning ℚ(√3,√5,i) (T14); at five colours 25 of K(√7)(i)'s 28 quartic CM subfields are loose for free and the pilot ℚ(√3,√−77) by a gap at `N = 7`. **The rich 2-unit lattices over ℚ(√3,√5)** (generator graphs; saturation unaffordable): `F_known = 0`, `P_found` of rank 8 and finite index, every colouring found gap-free, `4/3, 8/3, 4, 4/7, 4/√3 ∈ P_found` — **rigid-found, forcing undecided**, partly by construction (Poros); `N = 5, 7` inadmissible (`1/p ∈ Λ`). **Rank 6:** L3 is loose (`F = P = 0`) **without a gap** — one period-2 colouring with trivial Kempe period group, so G68's gap is sufficient for looseness, not necessary; L2, L6 loose by gaps; **[hand] the fibre-collision sandwich** `D_Q ⊆ V_Q` (a target escapes iff it misses `V_Q`: membership, not the index; L3's `81 = 3⁴` is arithmetic of its unit vectors mod 2); **KR6** (de Grey's lattice + the ζ₆-orbit of ρ₁₅, rank 6, 78 unit vectors) has `F ⊇ 8Λ_dG` and no periodic colouring at `N ≤ 6`, **its four-colourability open**; chiral non-additive colourings are common and all sit on loose lattices. **[kernel]** `Proofs/HadwigerNelson/Kempe.lean`, zero `sorry`, standard three axioms: Kempe swaps (`kempeSwap`, `not_forces_of_kempeClosed`), **`forces_iff_exists_finite`** (a pair is forced iff a finite induced subgraph forces it; de Bruijn–Erdős by `Finset.rado_selection`), the voltage-escape certificate (`not_forces_of_voltageEscape`, FS70-T10.1), `forces_iff_of_quotient_fanGap` (FS70-T6), `not_forces_of_additiveKempe` (FS70-T2), `forcedSubgroup` / `periodicForcedSubgroup`, residual finiteness of finitely generated abelian groups through characters (`exists_finiteIndex_between_notMem`), **`notMem_periodicForcedSubgroup_of_voltageEscape`** (R3's escape direction), `InheritanceQuestion` (a `Prop`, stated, never claimed) with `inheritanceQuestion_iff`, the inhabitant `voltageEscapeZ` — priced at 1½ days, closed in about 40 minutes. **Predictions** (`plans/FS73-rigidity-dichotomy.md` §P, scored in §R): won P-C1, P-C2, P-C6, P-T1, P-R1 (index-81 component), P-F1, P-F2, P-F4; lost P-S1, P-C4, P-C3(b); P-C5 decided (`P = F`); void P-C3(a), P-F3 (unreachable at registration); P-T3 won on the verdict, lost on the mechanism. **Prior art (found at the close, in the archived Polymath16 threads we hold):** **the headline count is Hubai's** (`[Hubai2018]`, fourth thread, 9 May 2018, by computer search, no certificate): the Moser ring `R₂` — as his computation uses it, closed under rotation by `η = ω₃^{1/2}` — has exactly **9,120 four-colourings, 380 with a triangle fixed**, every one unchanged by translation by 8, each restricting on the Eisenstein sublattice to two-colour stripes and fixed by one chirality choice; - LEDGER.md:124 (row 79): | 79 | **Three colours are linear: every Cayley graph of a finitely generated torsion-free abelian group is 3-colourable iff a character puts every generator in `[1/3, 2/3]`, its periodic 3-colourings are dense, and forced pairs are exactly what periodic colourings see** (FS-73's competition; the theorems `plans/FS73-derivation.md` T21–T30, T39–T45; the understanding is G71). **[hand; two independent derivations — de Bruijn and Coxeter — read by Poros; the owner checked the two deciding steps]** For `Λ` finitely generated torsion-free and `S = −S` finite generating: **(criterion)** `Cay(Λ, S)` is 3-colourable ⟺ `Θ₃ = {θ ∈ Hom(Λ, ℝ/ℤ) : θ(S) ⊆ [1/3, 2/3]} ≠ ∅` ⟺ it has a periodic (circular) 3-colouring — every 3-colouring has an integer height through `K₃`'s universal cover whose winding is realised by a point of `Θ₃`; **(density)** periodic 3-colourings are dense, so **`P = F` (C3-inherit) and C3-aper hold at three colours for the whole class, with no arithmetic of the lattice**; **(dichotomy)** `F` has finite index ⟺ the colouring space is finite ⟺ every cell of `Θ₃` is a point, and if every point has order 3 every 3-colouring is affine. This answers `[AbrishamiEtAl2025]` Problem 4.6 at `χ = 3` for these graphs, and reproduces `[FourUnitVectors2025]` Thm 1.2 in one line. **[tools, verified + exact] ℚ(√11)'s `Λ₁₀`** (the 36 unit vectors of denominator dividing 10): **`F = P = 3Λ`**, first by FS-58's two forcers (52 and 65 points, both needed) and ten separators (independently verified: drat-trim and exhaustive enumeration), then **derived with no forcer and no solver**: `Θ₃(Λ₁₀)` is exactly 16 isolated points of order 3, so all 48 three-colourings are affine (one orbit) and `F = ⋂ ker φ = 3Λ`; the 100-vector `Λ₅₀` has `F = P = 3Λ₅₀` (replicated, not independently verified). **[cert] The `Θ`-emptiness certificate** — an exact tree with Farkas leaves and a Fraction-only checker that re-enumerates the lattice's unit vectors from its key: FS-61's ℚ(√47) shape is not 3-colourable in 2,997 nodes (653 KB) against a refusing ball's LRAT of tens of MB; `Θ₃` agrees with the solver record on nine lattices and with a transfer-matrix decider on 2,299 rank-1 sets. **ℚ(√167)** (the conjecture's cheapest exposed prediction, ≥ 6, against a proven 3 before this row): **[cert]** `L3e1c94c6` — P2's "no periodic colouring" — has a gapped residue colouring of period 23 at `(23, √167 − 11)`, its 36 unit vectors reaching half the residue circle; 52 of FS-61's 57 lattices carry certified gapped periodic three-colourings and 3 are 3-integral, so **FS-61's null at 167 is explained** — its one ask that could refuse (the one lattice reaching degree 14 under a 2M-point cap, 1,474,541 points, three-coloured in 3.66 s) is a noisy circular colouring (T20), and its reading that 167's 3-non-integral lattices hold too few unit vectors (20–36 against 11's 108) held for the lattices it asked, while its two richest (44 and 60 unit vectors) are the two the screens left open; the residue sets at 23 that colour are classified (224 classes, 98 colour, every one with a gap; the smallest refusing set a `μ₆` coset) and at 43 need ≥ 5 pairs to refuse — a design rule; **[exact]** `Ld14c588b` is 3-colourable by a linear form of period index 1,080 (Coxeter; the owner verified), past every period the searches reached; **[cert + hand] and `L21cc6506` (60 unit vectors, denominator 420) has `Θ₃ = ∅`, so it is not 3-colourable and `χ(ℚ(√167)²) ≥ 4`** — the first bound above three where the conjecture predicts six (FS-59's first target; at four colours `L21cc6506` is four-colourable and loose, row 81) — by two independent exact certificates: implementer E's two routes (a θ-space tree of 605,203 nodes, 7.7 MB gzipped, accepted by Coxeter's unmodified checker and by E's own; and a structural certificate over the product `U = {1, v, v̄, z, z̄}·U_R`, 4,768 Farkas-refuted cases, 106 KB, with its own standalone checker) and verifier V2's relation-branching tree (written before reading E: 30,344 leaves over 39 relations, 149 KB gzipped, `data/fs73/verify-v2/L21cc6506-empty.json.gz`, a second structurally different tree also passing), each checked by a Fraction/int-only checker that re-enumerates the lattice's unit vectors (`tools/fs73v2_check.py`: the owner re-ran it, PASS, and five planted faults were each refused for its own reason; a fake-empty certificate for a colourable lattice is refused), with the criterion's ⟹ direction (T22) derived independently by both competitors, read by Poros and the owner, and re-derived a third way by V2 (a forward `{1, 2}` lift: no parity step, no generation hypothesis). **A half-page proof exists** (E, checked line by line by V2): the 36 unit vectors `{1, v, z}·U_R` alone have empty `Θ₃` — a minimal obstruction — by four inequality cases. **No finite non-3-colourable graph is exhibited**; compactness guarantees one. **[hand] Four colours have neither, cell by cell** — `K₄`'s square complex is `ℝP²`, whose one loop invariant, a ℤ/2 winding, is fixed by the graph (the relation's parity); an edge-local height exists only on four-colourable presentations of rank ≤ 2 (Moore–Newman's triangular lattice among them), and no edge-local height, hence no slope of that kind, on any other presentation (both row 80); `G(ℤ, {1,3,4,5})` is 4-colourable with no linear colouring — so no `Θ`-type certificate built from edge-local heights exists at four colours, and a two-sided finite certificate scheme exists iff 4-colourability of Cayley graphs of ℤ^r is decidable (stated open in print: `[CGHO2025bg]` §6.2.1). Rank-1 four-colour walls (first at `max D = 10`) all sit where `P = F = 0`. **Predictions:** P-T2 lost at P2 to an outcome it did not name, then answered by C (loose on 52 of 57); Poros's hunches "both competitors drift to rigidity" and "no inheritance theorem" killed (the latter at three colours); P-Q1 ("not landed" 0.60, theorem 0.10) — a theorem at three colours, not landed at four; E's P3b prediction (`Θ₃(L21cc6506)` nonempty, 0.60) lost. **Prior art:** the criterion and density answer, at `k = 3` and every rank, a question posed three times — `[Ellenberg2018]` (MathOverflow, 2018: does a `k`-colourable Cayley graph of ℤ^r have a periodic `k`-colouring?), P. Gibbs and D. Mixon on Polymath16 (2018; `[Polymath16threads]`), carried as open by `[CGHO2025bg]` §6.2.1 (2025) — `CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §2.4, with the scope of that check; the height lift is the standard device for four-cycle-free targets (Chandgotia 2017, K. Schmidt's cocycles); circular colourings of distance graphs are classical; density at the *standard* generators is expected folklore (Chandgotia–Marcus 2018, listing) and not claimed; rank 1 (`G(ℤ, D)` 3-colourable iff `κ(D) ≥ 1/3`) not found stated. **Ours:** the arbitrary-generator criterion through the relation lattice, density and the dichotomy for every such graph, `F = 3Λ` derived, the `Θ` certificate and checker, the ℚ(√167) screen and its colourings, **`χ(ℚ(√167)²) ≥ 4`**, the four-colour obstruction's statement. **Not claimed:** `χ(ℚ(√167)²) ≥ 5` (row 123's); that `χ(ℚ(√167)²) ≥ 4` is new (not found in print: zbMATH and the held Polymath16 threads, FS-73's close); anything at four colours beyond the obstruction; C3-aper at `k ≥ 4`; the kernel form, which is row 119 (FS-75). | `plans/FS73-C3-deBruijn.md`, `plans/FS73-C3-Coxeter.md`, `plans/POROS-2026-09-23-fs73-competition.md`, `plans/FS73-derivation.md`, `data/fs73/records/` (`phase-P2.md`, `verify-P2-q11.md`, `phase-P2b-q167.md`), `data/fs73/c3-debruijn/`, `data/fs73/coxeter-c3/` (`certtree.py`, `certcheck.py`, `cert_47.json`), `data/fs73/q167/`, `data/fs73/verify-v2/`, `data/fs73/records/phase-P3b-q167-theta.md`, `data/fs73/records/verify-P3b-q167.md`, `tools/fs73_q167{,_check}.py`, `tools/fs73_theta_*.py`, `tools/fs73v2_*.py`, `tools/fs73v_q11.py` | FS-73 |

- LEDGER.md:174 (row 129): | 129 | **Row 123's five is a fractional bound rounded, and given MR the fractional local–global principle holds exactly. Row 123's chain runs one colour class at a time; the colour count enters only at the pigeonhole on the densest class, so its bound is `χ(F²) ≥ ⌈1/α_loc(F)⌉` with `α_loc(F) = max(m₁(ℝ²), sup_v r_v)` (`r_v` a stable place's all-levels independence ratio) and `1/α_loc ≤ 4.36`: that route never gives six. Given MR(F), every `R ⋊ U`-invariant random independent set of `F²` has density at most `α_loc(F)`, so the finitary independence ratio is `α₁(F²) = α_loc(F)`; unconditionally a denser invariant set gives an SP witness. At ℚ(√635087), given MR there: `α₁(ℝ²) = m₁(ℝ²)`, i.e. `f(n)/n → m₁(ℝ²)` — the first clause of `[DuczVarga2026]` Conjecture 1 — and `χ_f,0(ℝ²) = χ_f(ℝ²) = 1/m₁(ℝ²) ∈ [4.04876, 4.35987]`, answering `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]` §2's "it is not known whether equality holds" — posed first by `[Johnson1996fcn]` (p. 103, Question 1) and `[Johnson2000surprises]` (p. 143, Problem 3), read 2026-09-27; finite triangle-free unit-distance graphs reach it. At ℚ(√47), given MR there, held certificates give a triangle-free graph with `χ_f ≥ 4.0000439`** (FS-103; FS103-F-SI, EXT, FRAC, TJ, ROUND, DV, TF, 47, PL, B-AMEN, B-CHIF). **[hand + tools exact; FRAC, TJ, ROUND, DV, TF, 47 two derivations (FS103-F; FS103-B blind to F's record); B-AMEN's reverse half and B-CHIF one mind, both off the killing test's chain and off `α₁(ℝ²) = m₁(ℝ²)`, which use only B-AMEN's Følner half (two blind: F, B; FS105-W-D1); the whole weight MR's (row 120)]** Mechanism: the published expectation `α₁(ℝ²) ≠ m₁(ℝ²)` (`[AmbrusEtAl2023]` §4; `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]`) came from graphs in the Moser lattice, where the tight dyadic places pin `1/4`; the gap is a place's, gone at a field whose places all read below `m₁` (G60). **A finite-graph killing test for MR:** any proof that `α₁(ℝ²) > m₁(ℝ²)` refutes MR at ℚ(√635087). Such a proof must be (W) a bound `α(G) ≥ c|G|` over every finite unit-distance graph with `c ∈ (0.24699…, 0.249956…)` — above `[AmbrusEtAl2023]`'s bound on `m₁`, below `[DuczVarga2026]`'s on `α₁`, a window `0.00297` wide and `0.0176` above the best proved lower bound on `α₁` — or (W′) `χ_f,0(ℝ²) < χ_f(ℝ²)`, through B-CHIF; its cheap form `α₁ ≥ 1/4` was reachable in print and is false (FS105-W-D1, W-D2). **Not claimed:** `m₁ = δ_Croft` (the conjecture's second clause); any value of `m₁`; anything at six. **Prior art:** `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]` Conjecture 1 was already disproved in print by `[DuczVarga2026]` (held, read); the equalities not found (F §3.6, five queries; a gate under descriptive-combinatorics names owed); B-CHIF likely folklore. Notation: F's `ρ(F)` is renamed `α_loc(F)` against MRVZ's Hall ratio `ρ(ℝ²) = 1/α₁`. | `plans/FS103-derivation.md` §2; `plans/FS103-F-fractional-shadow.md` §2–§3; `plans/FS103-B-verification.md` §2–§4; `tools/fs103_f_places.py`, `tools/fs103_b_places.py`; `plans/FS105-derivation.md` §4 | FS-103 (2026-09-26) |
